The approved plan

I'll read the source first.

REGIONS: qmult.$mul$conv_layer.v:2854$339
SHARED_REDUCTION: seq_mac's accumulator add_out_reg (the qadd sum), one per seq_mac instance
BLOCKS: 84
MAPPING: per seq_mac instance (84 instances, one block each): A = {11'b0, a_flopped}, B = {2'b0, b_flopped}, C = {31'b0, cload_reg} (new 17-bit soft register, non-zero only on the cycle after an eltwise-add edge), OPMODE = constant 9'b010110101 (P <= P + C + A2*B2), CLK = clk, RSTA = RSTB = reset, RSTP = reset | (eltwise_mode & eltwise_add), CEA2 = CEB2 = CEP = 1'b1; the visible add_out_reg = P[31:0] | {15'b0, cload_reg}, which feeds the unchanged saturation/downcast logic and out

PLAN:

What the measurement showed. The kept rewrite's critical path is the per-cycle OPMODE path: the control flop that drives op (0.060) + 1.702 ns of routing to the PE + one LUT (0.153, the ~sel term) + 1.314 ns of routing + the block's 2.140 + 0.019 setup = 5.41 ns. OPMODE and C are the only block inputs whose path is not cut at the block pin; everything driven from a global control signal into OPMODE therefore pays routing-to-the-PE plus the full 2.159 ns block delay. RSTA/RSTB/RSTP/CEx/A/B, by contrast, end at a block register (setup 0.019). This revision keeps the same block and the same 84 instances but moves all dependence on eltwise_mode/eltwise_add off OPMODE and onto RSTP (a cheap endpoint) and onto a local register that drives C with no logic in between. OPMODE becomes a constant, so the long control route no longer runs through the block.

Candidate regions. One candidate, qmult.$mul$conv_layer.v:2854$339; converted in all 84 instances of qmult (one per seq_mac, instantiated from the PE at conv_layer.v:2648). None left. 84 blocks, one DSP tile each, within the device's 306; the unmodified design already uses 84 DSP tiles.

The idea. Write A(n) for the original add_out_reg during cycle n, r(n) for reset, s(n) for eltwise_mode & eltwise_add, S(n) = a_flopped(n)+b_flopped(n) (17 bits), M(n) for mul_out_reg(n). The original (lines 2804-2818) is

A(n+1) = r(n) ? 0 : ( s(n) ? S(n) : A(n) + M(n) ).

Instead of switching OPMODE between accumulate and load-C, clear the block's accumulator with RSTP on an eltwise-add edge and carry the loaded value S(n) in a soft 17-bit register cload_reg for the one cycle it is live; the block adds it back into the accumulator at the next edge through C, with the constant OPMODE P <= P + C + A2*B2. Because cload_reg is non-zero only on a cycle where RSTP fired at the previous edge, P is 0 exactly on those cycles, so the architectural value is P | cload_reg — a bitwise OR, not an adder (the two are never both non-zero). The 32-bit soft adder and the 32-bit mux of the original stay deleted.

Formally, with RSTP = r|s, OPMODE constant, C = {31'b0, cload_reg}:

P(n+1) = (r(n)|s(n)) ? 0 : P(n) + cload_reg(n) + M(n) (48-bit, low 32 bits used)
cload_reg(n+1) = r(n) ? 0 : ( s(n) ? S(n) : 0 )
V(n) = P(n)[31:0] | {15'b0, cload_reg(n)}

Invariants: cload_reg(n) != 0 implies s(n-1) & ~r(n-1), which implies RSTP was high at edge n-1, so P(n) = 0; hence V(n) = P(n) + cload_reg(n) always. Induction on V(n) = A(n):

  • r(n)=1: P(n+1)=0, cload_reg(n+1)=0, V=0 = A(n+1).
  • r(n)=0, s(n)=1: P(n+1)=0 (RSTP), cload_reg(n+1)=S(n), V = S(n) = A(n+1).
  • r(n)=0, s(n)=0: P(n+1) = P(n)+cload_reg(n)+M(n) ≡ A(n)+M(n), cload_reg(n+1)=0, V(n+1) = A(n+1). The block adds modulo 2^48 and the design modulo 2^32; the low 32 bits of a sum depend only on the low 32 bits of the addends, and only P[31:0] is read, so the wrap matches.

M(n) = A2(n)*B2(n) equals the original mul_out_reg(n) because RSTA/RSTB = reset clears A2/B2 on exactly the edges that cleared mul_out_reg (and 0*0 = 0), and CEA2/CEB2 = 1 as mul_out_reg always loaded.

Edits for the editor, inside module seq_mac only (conv_layer.v:2765-2846).

  1. Keep the a_flopped/b_flopped always block (lines 2783-2791) exactly as it is.
  2. Delete: the qmult instance (line 2793); mul_out/mul_out_reg and their always block (lines 2780-2781, 2795-2801); add_in1/add_in2 and their assigns (lines 2804-2808); the qadd instance (line 2810); add_out (line 2775); the add_out_reg register and its always block (lines 2806, 2812-2818). Leave the qmult and qadd module definitions (lines 2849-2866) in the file; nothing else instantiates them.
  3. Add, in place of the deleted logic:
    ```verilog
    wire sel = eltwise_mode & eltwise_add;

//carries the eltwise-add load value for the one cycle it is live
reg [`DWIDTH:0] cload_reg;
always @(posedge clk) begin
if (reset) cload_reg <= 0;
else if (sel) cload_reg <= {1'b0, a_flopped} + {1'b0, b_flopped};
else cload_reg <= 0;
end

wire [47:0] dsp_p;
dsp48e2_macc mac_dsp (
.CLK(clk),
.A({{(27-`DWIDTH){1'b0}}, a_flopped}),
.B({{(18-`DWIDTH){1'b0}}, b_flopped}),
.C({{(48-`DWIDTH-1){1'b0}}, cload_reg}),
.OPMODE(9'b010110101),
.RSTA(reset), .RSTB(reset), .RSTP(reset | sel),
.CEA2(1'b1), .CEB2(1'b1), .CEP(1'b1),
.P(dsp_p)
);

wire [2*`DWIDTH-1:0] add_out_reg =
dsp_p[2*DWIDTH-1:0] | {{(DWIDTH-1){1'b0}}, cload_reg};

`OPMODE` is the literal constant `9'b010110101`, a legal value of the mode (`P <= P + C + A2*B2`), tied the same on every cycle, so `P` is never X and no design signal reaches `OPMODE`.

4. Leave the `mac_out` saturation assign (lines 2823-2835) and the `out` register (lines 2838-2844) untouched; they read the new `add_out_reg` wire.

**Register stages, operands to result.** MAC path before: `a`/`b` → `a_flopped`/`b_flopped` (1) → multiply → `mul_out_reg` (2) → add/mux → `add_out_reg` (3) → saturation → `out` (4). After: `a`/`b` → `a_flopped`/`b_flopped` soft (1) → block `A2`/`B2` (2) → block `P` (3) → OR + saturation → `out` soft (4). Same four stages. Eltwise-add path before: `a`/`b` → `a_flopped`/`b_flopped` (1) → zero-extend/add/mux → `add_out_reg` (2) → saturation → `out` (3). After: `a`/`b` → `a_flopped`/`b_flopped` (1) → 17-bit add → `cload_reg` (2) → OR + saturation → `out` (3). Same three stages; the loaded value never passes through `P`.

**Why this should be faster.** The only paths into the block that carry design logic are now: `C`, driven straight off `cload_reg` with no intervening LUT (flop 0.060 + one route + 2.159); and `RSTP`/`CEx`/`A`/`B`, which end at a block register (the long control route + one LUT + 0.019 ≈ 3.3 ns worst case on `reset | sel`). The 5.41 ns `OPMODE`-through-the-block path disappears. The new output-side term is the 17-bit OR between `P` and the saturation logic; its low-bit OR merges into the saturation mux LUT and its high bits merge into the existing `|add_out_reg[30:15]` reduction, so the logic depth from `P` to `out` should be unchanged.

**Area.** Versus the kept candidate: `sum_reg` (17 flops, unconditional load) is replaced by `cload_reg` (17 flops, enable plus zero-load) and its 17-bit adder moves from in front of the register to in front of it as well — the same order of soft logic; the added OR is 17 LUT inputs that fold into existing LUTs. No 32-bit soft adder, no 32-bit mux and no 32-bit `add_out_reg` register return. DSP tiles stay at 84.

**Correctness notes.** Visibility: `mul_out` and `add_out` have no reader outside the registers that consumed them (lines 2780-2818), so nothing hidden in the block is read elsewhere; `a_flopped`/`b_flopped` remain soft registers because `A2`/`B2` are not readable and `cload_reg`'s adder needs them; the architectural `add_out_reg` leaves the block on `P` (ORed with `cload_reg`). Resets: `RSTA`/`RSTB`/`RSTP` are driven so the block registers clear on exactly the edges the original's registers cleared, and `RSTP` wins over `CEP` just as the original's `if (reset)` won over its mux; mid-run resets of arbitrary length are covered by the induction above, which is over every cycle. Power-up/X: block registers start at 0; `cload_reg` is a new soft register and starts unknown, but it is cleared at every edge of the testbench's 4-cycle reset phase and can reach `P` only through `C`, while `RSTP` is high at each of those edges, so no X reaches `P` or `out`, and comparison begins after the reset release. Widths: both multiplier operands are 16-bit unsigned, zero-extended, 16 ≤ 26 on `A` and 16 ≤ 17 on `B`, so each operand's top bit is 0 and the 32-bit product is exact; `C` is a zero-extended 17-bit value.

No assumption about the design's inputs is needed; the rewrite is unconditionally equivalent, including when `eltwise_mode`/`eltwise_add`/`reset` toggle arbitrarily.

  • one dsp48e2_macc mac_dsp with A = {11'b0, a_flopped}, B = {2'b0, b_flopped}, C = {31'b0, cload_reg}, constant OPMODE = 9'b010110101 (P <= P + C + A2*B2), RSTA = RSTB = reset, RSTP = reset | sel, CEA2 = CEB2 = CEP = 1'b1, P to a new dsp_p[47:0]. Every input port is connected, by the block's port names.
  • wire [31:0] add_out_reg = dsp_p[31:0] | {15'b0, cload_reg}; — the architectural accumulator value, a bitwise OR since cload_reg != 0 only on cycles where RSTP fired at the previous edge and P is therefore 0.

Saturation/downcast and the out register. Untouched; they read the new add_out_reg wire.

Module definitions qmult and qadd (conv_layer.v, end of file). Left in place as the plan says; nothing instantiates them now, and no module named dsp48e2_macc is defined in the design.

No ASSUMES: line is needed — the rewrite is unconditionally equivalent, including under mid-run resets and arbitrary eltwise_mode/eltwise_add toggling.

Cell census: dsp48e2_macc 84, no $mul.

tool events 16 recorded, 0 error(s)

---
#!/usr/bin/env bash
# Inner-loop check: the front end of the real flow, then a cell census. NOT place-and-route.
# usage: bash check.sh <file.v>
# Reads, in the flow's order: blocks.vlib (every hard block of the architecture, declared from its
# models), primitives.vlib (the flow's library of memories, adders and multipliers), then the design;
# then elaborates with the flow's hierarchy check. A module that is not defined, a port name the
# block does not have, or a misspelled block name fails here as it would in the flow. Do not
# declare the target block in the design: blocks.vlib declares it, and a design that defines a module
# of the target block's name fails this check.
# Prints one pass/fail line, then the cell census of the whole design (the last "Number of cells"
# table yosys prints): soft arithmetic as $mul/$add/$sub, registers as $dff*/$sdff*, memories as
# $mem*, muxes as $mux/$pmux, and every instantiated module by name, which is where a hard-block
# instantiation shows up with its instance count.
set -u
f="${1:?usage: check.sh <file.v>}"
here="$(cd "$(dirname "$0")" && pwd)"
lib=""
[ -f "$here/blocks.vlib" ] && lib="read_verilog -lib $here/blocks.vlib; "
[ -f "$here/primitives.vlib" ] && lib="${lib}read_verilog -nomem2reg $here/primitives.vlib; "
target="$(sed -n 's|^// target block: *\([A-Za-z_][A-Za-z0-9_$]*\).*|\1|p' "$here/blocks.vlib" 2>/dev/null | head -1)"
if [ -n "$target" ] && grep -Eq "^[[:space:]]*module[[:space:]]+$target([^A-Za-z0-9_$]|$)" "$f"; then
  echo "FAIL: $f defines module $target; blocks.vlib declares it. Remove the module definition and keep the instances."
  exit 1
fi
out="$(yosys -p "${lib}read_verilog -sv -nolatches $f; hierarchy -check -auto-top -purge_lib; proc; opt; stat" 2>&1)"
rc=$?
if [ $rc -ne 0 ]; then
  echo "FAIL: does not synthesize (yosys exit $rc)"
  echo "$out" | grep -iE 'error|warning' | tail -15
  echo "$out" | tail -10
  exit 1
fi
echo "OK: synthesizes cleanly"
echo "Cell census (yosys stat, whole design):"
echo "$out" | awk '/Number of cells:/{buf=$0 "\n"; on=1; next}
                   on && /^[[:space:]]+[$A-Za-z_][^[:space:]]*[[:space:]]+[0-9]+[[:space:]]*$/ {buf=buf $0 "\n"; next}
                   on {on=0}
                   END{printf "%s", buf}' \
  | grep -E 'Number of cells|^[[:space:]]+(\$(mul|add|sub|mem|memrd|memwr|dff|dffe|sdff|sdffe|adff|adffe|mux|pmux)|[A-Za-z_])' || true
---

module seq_mac(a, b, out, eltwise_mode, eltwise_add, reset, clk);
input [`DWIDTH-1:0] a;
input [`DWIDTH-1:0] b;
output [`DWIDTH-1:0] out;
input eltwise_mode;
input eltwise_add;
input reset;
input clk;

reg [`DWIDTH-1:0] out;

reg [`DWIDTH-1:0] a_flopped;
reg [`DWIDTH-1:0] b_flopped;

always @(posedge clk) begin
  if (reset) begin
    a_flopped <= 0;
    b_flopped <= 0;
  end else begin
    a_flopped <= a;
    b_flopped <= b;
  end
end

wire sel = eltwise_mode & eltwise_add;

//carries the eltwise-add load value for the one cycle it is live
reg [`DWIDTH:0] cload_reg;
always @(posedge clk) begin
  if (reset)    cload_reg <= 0;
  else if (sel) cload_reg <= {1'b0, a_flopped} + {1'b0, b_flopped};
  else          cload_reg <= 0;
end

wire [47:0] dsp_p;
dsp48e2_macc mac_dsp (
    .CLK(clk),

Task

You are the acceptance reviewer. You did not plan or write this edit. Check the edit against the approved plan, the block's contract and the correctness rules.

The block

This run offered 2 blocks (dsp48e2_macc and dsp48e2_preadd_ma). Its first approved plan chose dsp48e2_macc, described below, and the choice is fixed for the rest of the run. The edit must instantiate only that block.

  • primitive to instantiate: dsp48e2_macc (precision INT27)
  • what it is: this mode copies the AMD DSP48E2 block in one configuration, the one-slice "27x18 + C MULT/MACC" row of UG579 v1.11 Table 2-12 (p46); OPMODE Tables 2-3 to 2-6 (p30). It is not the whole DSP48E2: only the ports below exist, and the rest of the real block is fixed inside the mode (ALUMODE = 0000, INMODE = 00000, CARRYINSEL = 000, CARRYIN = 0, D = 0, A[29:27] = 0, every cascade input 0, every clock enable and reset not listed as a port 0). Differences from the real block: one slice per tile where AMD's tile has two; A is 27 bits: A[29:27] serve only the A:B path, which is outside the mode; OPMODE is restricted to the listed values; PCOUT, ACOUT, BCOUT, the carry, pattern-detect and XOR outputs are not exposed; timing figures and tile area are this project's, not AMD's.
  • computes: P = A*B + C. That is one of the forms the block takes: OPMODE chooses the form at every clock edge (listed below).
  • input ports: CLK[1] (clock), A[27], B[18], C[48], OPMODE[9], RSTA[1], RSTB[1], RSTP[1], CEA2[1], CEB2[1], CEP[1]
  • output ports: P[48]
  • arithmetic: the block's operands and result are signed two's complement. A design operand narrower than its port must be widened to the port width: zero-extended if the design treats it as unsigned, sign-extended if signed. For unsigned data the top bit of each multiplier operand must be zero (UG579 v1.11 p38: "The multiplier can emulate unsigned math by setting the MSB of an input operand to zero."). AMD's words end with that sentence; the limits that follow are this project's, derived from it. So an unsigned operand may be at most 26 bits on A and 17 bits on B; a wider unsigned operand has no exact representation on this block. C and P are 48-bit values added modulo 2^48; extend a narrower addend to 48 bits by its own signedness. The result wraps at 48 bits: a value past the result width keeps its low 48 bits (modulo 2^48), with no saturation and no flag.
  • registers: on CLK, the block has exactly these registers. A2 loads A at a clock edge where CEA2 is high, and is cleared to 0 by RSTA. B2 loads B at a clock edge where CEB2 is high, and is cleared to 0 by RSTB. P loads the value OPMODE selects from A2*B2, C and P at a clock edge where CEP is high, and is cleared to 0 by RSTP; the output P is this register. P is always registered; C and OPMODE reach P without an input register.
  • timing: A and B reach P 2 clock edges after they are applied (applied before edge k, in P after edge k+1); C and OPMODE have no register before the one that drives P: the value present at a clock edge is in P after that edge (1 cycle).

  • control OPMODE: a per-cycle input, not registered; it acts at the clock edge that loads P, with the C value present at that edge. Legal values, and what the clock edge then does:

    • 000000101 (multiply): P <= A2*B2
    • 000100101 (multiply-accumulate): P <= P + A2*B2
    • 000110101 (multiply-add): P <= C + A2*B2
    • 010110101 (multiply-add with the running sum): P <= P + C + A2*B2
    • 110000000 (load C): P <= C
    • 000110000 (load C (second encoding)): P <= C
    • 010000000 (hold): P <= P
    • 000000000 (zero): P <= 0
    • any other value: a clock edge that loads P under it leaves P unknown (X); the mode does not define it.
  • resets and clock enables (resets RSTA, RSTB and RSTP; clock enables CEA2, CEB2 and CEP): each reset is synchronous and active high, clears only its own register to 0 at the clock edge that samples it high, and wins over that register's clock enable; each clock enable is active high; its register holds its value at a clock edge that samples the enable low. The block has no other reset and no other enable.
  • power-up: every register of the block starts at 0 (as the vendor's simulation model starts them), so P is defined from the first clock edge with or without a reset.
  • connections: connect every input port, by the port names above. A reset the rewrite does not need is tied to 0; a clock enable it does not need is tied to 1; OPMODE may be a constant legal value.
The block on this architecture

  • Blocks in series are separate stages. A path into C and OPMODE is not cut at the block's pins: it runs through the block to the register behind P, so the logic that drives them and the block's delay are in one stage.
  • A block of this mode takes a DSP tile alone.

Correctness: these must hold
  • Same behavior. The rewritten design must produce the same outputs as the original on every cycle, with every register of the original starting at 0. Do not change what the design computes. Logic added only to reproduce it (recomputing a value, deriving the block's resets, clock enables or per-cycle control, correcting a block's result) is allowed.
  • Visibility. Only what the block's output ports carry is visible outside it. A value read by anything else (another expression, a module port, another module) cannot go inside a block: keep it outside, or recompute it. Check the source for every reader.
  • Terms. Keep every term of the original arithmetic. A term with no matching block port is computed outside the block. A port the design ties to a constant may be tied the same way on the block.
  • Registers and reset. The block's registers, if it has any, behave as the contract states. A design register may become a block register only if that keeps its behavior. Drive the block's resets (RSTA, RSTB and RSTP), clock enables (CEA2, CEB2 and CEP) and per-cycle control (OPMODE) so that "same behavior" holds after every reset of the design, not only at start-up. Registers the rewrite adds in soft logic start unknown; do not rely on their initial value. The block's own registers start at 0, as its power-up line states.
  • Assumptions. If the rewrite is correct only under an assumption about the design's inputs or use (a bound on a value, when a signal is asserted), state it on a line starting ASSUMES: and cite the lines of the design that support it. The reference testbench does not know your assumption, so the rewrite must still match the original on the testbench's stimulus, which does not respect it.

The approved plan

I'll read the source first.

REGIONS: qmult.$mul$conv_layer.v:2854$339
SHARED_REDUCTION: seq_mac's accumulator add_out_reg (the qadd sum), one per seq_mac instance
BLOCKS: 84
MAPPING: per seq_mac instance (84 instances, one block each): A = {11'b0, a_flopped}, B = {2'b0, b_flopped}, C = {31'b0, cload_reg} (new 17-bit soft register, non-zero only on the cycle after an eltwise-add edge), OPMODE = constant 9'b010110101 (P <= P + C + A2*B2), CLK = clk, RSTA = RSTB = reset, RSTP = reset | (eltwise_mode & eltwise_add), CEA2 = CEB2 = CEP = 1'b1; the visible add_out_reg = P[31:0] | {15'b0, cload_reg}, which feeds the unchanged saturation/downcast logic and out

PLAN:

What the measurement showed. The kept rewrite's critical path is the per-cycle OPMODE path: the control flop that drives op (0.060) + 1.702 ns of routing to the PE + one LUT (0.153, the ~sel term) + 1.314 ns of routing + the block's 2.140 + 0.019 setup = 5.41 ns. OPMODE and C are the only block inputs whose path is not cut at the block pin; everything driven from a global control signal into OPMODE therefore pays routing-to-the-PE plus the full 2.159 ns block delay. RSTA/RSTB/RSTP/CEx/A/B, by contrast, end at a block register (setup 0.019). This revision keeps the same block and the same 84 instances but moves all dependence on eltwise_mode/eltwise_add off OPMODE and onto RSTP (a cheap endpoint) and onto a local register that drives C with no logic in between. OPMODE becomes a constant, so the long control route no longer runs through the block.

Candidate regions. One candidate, qmult.$mul$conv_layer.v:2854$339; converted in all 84 instances of qmult (one per seq_mac, instantiated from the PE at conv_layer.v:2648). None left. 84 blocks, one DSP tile each, within the device's 306; the unmodified design already uses 84 DSP tiles.

The idea. Write A(n) for the original add_out_reg during cycle n, r(n) for reset, s(n) for eltwise_mode & eltwise_add, S(n) = a_flopped(n)+b_flopped(n) (17 bits), M(n) for mul_out_reg(n). The original (lines 2804-2818) is

A(n+1) = r(n) ? 0 : ( s(n) ? S(n) : A(n) + M(n) ).

Instead of switching OPMODE between accumulate and load-C, clear the block's accumulator with RSTP on an eltwise-add edge and carry the loaded value S(n) in a soft 17-bit register cload_reg for the one cycle it is live; the block adds it back into the accumulator at the next edge through C, with the constant OPMODE P <= P + C + A2*B2. Because cload_reg is non-zero only on a cycle where RSTP fired at the previous edge, P is 0 exactly on those cycles, so the architectural value is P | cload_reg — a bitwise OR, not an adder (the two are never both non-zero). The 32-bit soft adder and the 32-bit mux of the original stay deleted.

Formally, with RSTP = r|s, OPMODE constant, C = {31'b0, cload_reg}:

P(n+1) = (r(n)|s(n)) ? 0 : P(n) + cload_reg(n) + M(n) (48-bit, low 32 bits used)
cload_reg(n+1) = r(n) ? 0 : ( s(n) ? S(n) : 0 )
V(n) = P(n)[31:0] | {15'b0, cload_reg(n)}

Invariants: cload_reg(n) != 0 implies s(n-1) & ~r(n-1), which implies RSTP was high at edge n-1, so P(n) = 0; hence V(n) = P(n) + cload_reg(n) always. Induction on V(n) = A(n):

  • r(n)=1: P(n+1)=0, cload_reg(n+1)=0, V=0 = A(n+1).
  • r(n)=0, s(n)=1: P(n+1)=0 (RSTP), cload_reg(n+1)=S(n), V = S(n) = A(n+1).
  • r(n)=0, s(n)=0: P(n+1) = P(n)+cload_reg(n)+M(n) ≡ A(n)+M(n), cload_reg(n+1)=0, V(n+1) = A(n+1). The block adds modulo 2^48 and the design modulo 2^32; the low 32 bits of a sum depend only on the low 32 bits of the addends, and only P[31:0] is read, so the wrap matches.

M(n) = A2(n)*B2(n) equals the original mul_out_reg(n) because RSTA/RSTB = reset clears A2/B2 on exactly the edges that cleared mul_out_reg (and 0*0 = 0), and CEA2/CEB2 = 1 as mul_out_reg always loaded.

Edits for the editor, inside module seq_mac only (conv_layer.v:2765-2846).

  1. Keep the a_flopped/b_flopped always block (lines 2783-2791) exactly as it is.
  2. Delete: the qmult instance (line 2793); mul_out/mul_out_reg and their always block (lines 2780-2781, 2795-2801); add_in1/add_in2 and their assigns (lines 2804-2808); the qadd instance (line 2810); add_out (line 2775); the add_out_reg register and its always block (lines 2806, 2812-2818). Leave the qmult and qadd module definitions (lines 2849-2866) in the file; nothing else instantiates them.
  3. Add, in place of the deleted logic:
    ```verilog
    wire sel = eltwise_mode & eltwise_add;

//carries the eltwise-add load value for the one cycle it is live
reg [`DWIDTH:0] cload_reg;
always @(posedge clk) begin
if (reset) cload_reg <= 0;
else if (sel) cload_reg <= {1'b0, a_flopped} + {1'b0, b_flopped};
else cload_reg <= 0;
end

wire [47:0] dsp_p;
dsp48e2_macc mac_dsp (
.CLK(clk),
.A({{(27-`DWIDTH){1'b0}}, a_flopped}),
.B({{(18-`DWIDTH){1'b0}}, b_flopped}),
.C({{(48-`DWIDTH-1){1'b0}}, cload_reg}),
.OPMODE(9'b010110101),
.RSTA(reset), .RSTB(reset), .RSTP(reset | sel),
.CEA2(1'b1), .CEB2(1'b1), .CEP(1'b1),
.P(dsp_p)
);

wire [2*`DWIDTH-1:0] add_out_reg =
dsp_p[2*DWIDTH-1:0] | {{(DWIDTH-1){1'b0}}, cload_reg};

`OPMODE` is the literal constant `9'b010110101`, a legal value of the mode (`P <= P + C + A2*B2`), tied the same on every cycle, so `P` is never X and no design signal reaches `OPMODE`.

4. Leave the `mac_out` saturation assign (lines 2823-2835) and the `out` register (lines 2838-2844) untouched; they read the new `add_out_reg` wire.

**Register stages, operands to result.** MAC path before: `a`/`b` → `a_flopped`/`b_flopped` (1) → multiply → `mul_out_reg` (2) → add/mux → `add_out_reg` (3) → saturation → `out` (4). After: `a`/`b` → `a_flopped`/`b_flopped` soft (1) → block `A2`/`B2` (2) → block `P` (3) → OR + saturation → `out` soft (4). Same four stages. Eltwise-add path before: `a`/`b` → `a_flopped`/`b_flopped` (1) → zero-extend/add/mux → `add_out_reg` (2) → saturation → `out` (3). After: `a`/`b` → `a_flopped`/`b_flopped` (1) → 17-bit add → `cload_reg` (2) → OR + saturation → `out` (3). Same three stages; the loaded value never passes through `P`.

**Why this should be faster.** The only paths into the block that carry design logic are now: `C`, driven straight off `cload_reg` with no intervening LUT (flop 0.060 + one route + 2.159); and `RSTP`/`CEx`/`A`/`B`, which end at a block register (the long control route + one LUT + 0.019 ≈ 3.3 ns worst case on `reset | sel`). The 5.41 ns `OPMODE`-through-the-block path disappears. The new output-side term is the 17-bit OR between `P` and the saturation logic; its low-bit OR merges into the saturation mux LUT and its high bits merge into the existing `|add_out_reg[30:15]` reduction, so the logic depth from `P` to `out` should be unchanged.

**Area.** Versus the kept candidate: `sum_reg` (17 flops, unconditional load) is replaced by `cload_reg` (17 flops, enable plus zero-load) and its 17-bit adder moves from in front of the register to in front of it as well — the same order of soft logic; the added OR is 17 LUT inputs that fold into existing LUTs. No 32-bit soft adder, no 32-bit mux and no 32-bit `add_out_reg` register return. DSP tiles stay at 84.

**Correctness notes.** Visibility: `mul_out` and `add_out` have no reader outside the registers that consumed them (lines 2780-2818), so nothing hidden in the block is read elsewhere; `a_flopped`/`b_flopped` remain soft registers because `A2`/`B2` are not readable and `cload_reg`'s adder needs them; the architectural `add_out_reg` leaves the block on `P` (ORed with `cload_reg`). Resets: `RSTA`/`RSTB`/`RSTP` are driven so the block registers clear on exactly the edges the original's registers cleared, and `RSTP` wins over `CEP` just as the original's `if (reset)` won over its mux; mid-run resets of arbitrary length are covered by the induction above, which is over every cycle. Power-up/X: block registers start at 0; `cload_reg` is a new soft register and starts unknown, but it is cleared at every edge of the testbench's 4-cycle reset phase and can reach `P` only through `C`, while `RSTP` is high at each of those edges, so no X reaches `P` or `out`, and comparison begins after the reset release. Widths: both multiplier operands are 16-bit unsigned, zero-extended, 16 ≤ 26 on `A` and 16 ≤ 17 on `B`, so each operand's top bit is 0 and the 32-bit product is exact; `C` is a zero-extended 17-bit value.

No assumption about the design's inputs is needed; the rewrite is unconditionally equivalent, including when `eltwise_mode`/`eltwise_add`/`reset` toggle arbitrarily.

DESIGN_CHANGE: if `seq_mac` sampled `eltwise_mode`/`eltwise_add` into a local register one cycle before the edge on which the eltwise load takes effect (as the PE's `ready_for_eltwise_op` sequencing at lines 2647-2656 effectively already guarantees), the mode select could drive `OPMODE` straight from a flop, letting the block's own load-C mode replace `cload_reg`, the `RSTP` term and the output OR.

ACCEPT

Checked against the plan, the dsp48e2_macc contract, and the correctness rules.

Plan conformance. The diff touches only seq_mac (conv_layer.v:2765-2840); the harness subtree check (sim_run.log:3) confirms everything outside qadd/qmult/seq_mac is unchanged, and qmult/qadd keep their definitions (conv_layer.v:2843-2860) as the plan directed. The deletions (mul_out, mul_out_reg, add_out, add_in1/2, the qmult/qadd instances, the 32-bit add_out_reg register) and the added sel, cload_reg, block instance and OR wire match the plan's edit list verbatim, including the constant OPMODE.

Contract. All 11 input ports are connected by name (conv_layer.v:2800-2809): CLK, A (11 zeros + 16 = 27), B (2 zeros + 16 = 18), C (31 zeros + 17 = 48), OPMODE = 9'b010110101, a legal value (P <= P + C + A2*B2, model line 41), RSTA/RSTB = reset, RSTP = reset|sel, CEA2/CEB2/CEP = 1. P drives dsp_p, which feeds only the add_out_reg wire → saturation → out, as the plan says. Both multiplier operands are zero-extended 16-bit unsigned (16 ≤ 26 on A, 16 ≤ 17 on B), so each top bit is 0 and the product is exact and non-negative.

Same behavior. A2(n)*B2(n) = a_flopped(n-1)*b_flopped(n-1) = mul_out_reg(n), since RSTA/RSTB clear on exactly the edges that cleared mul_out_reg and the enables are always high. cload_reg(n) != 0 implies ~r(n-1)&s(n-1), so RSTP fired at edge n-1 and P(n) = 0; the two OR'd terms are never both non-zero, so dsp_p[31:0] | cload_reg equals the architectural sum. Induction over the three cases (r, ~r&s, ~r&~s) reproduces A(n+1) = r ? 0 : (s ? a+b : A+M) for arbitrary toggling of reset/eltwise_*, including mid-run resets; the 48-bit wrap is invisible because only the low 32 bits are read. Register stage counts (4 on the MAC path, 3 on the eltwise-add path) are preserved, consistent with the undelayed comparison in sim_run.log:6.

Visibility. mul_out/mul_out_reg/add_out/add_in1/add_in2 had no reader outside the deleted logic (grep confirms no remaining references); a_flopped/b_flopped stay in soft logic because cload_reg's adder reads them.

X / power-up. cload_reg is the only added soft register; it is cleared whenever reset is high and out is reset-held on those same edges, so no X reaches out after reset release — the rewrite does not rely on its initial value. Block registers start at 0 per the contract.

No ASSUMES: line is needed and none is claimed; the equivalence is unconditional.

output
1	// dsp48e2_macc: behavioural model of the arch mode dsp48e2_macc_mode, which mimics the AMD DSP48E2 slice in the
2	// one-slice "27x18 + C MULT/MACC" operation mode (UG579 v1.11 Table 2-12, p46), attributes AREG = BREG = 1,
3	// ACASCREG = BCASCREG = 1, MREG = 0, PREG = 1, CREG = 0, OPMODEREG = 0, USE_MULT = MULTIPLY, AMULTSEL = A,
4	// BMULTSEL = B, no pre-adder, no cascade, no pattern detector, ALUMODE = 0000, INMODE = 00000,
5	// CARRYINSEL = 000, CARRYIN = 0. Reference for every per-cycle question: AMD's simulation model
6	// /tools/Xilinx/Vivado/2024.1/data/verilog/src/unisims/DSP48E2.v (Vivado 2024.1; registers start at 0 in the
7	// initial block at lines 1178-1217), read in place and never copied.
8	// Per clock edge: A2 <= A if CEA2 (cleared by RSTA); B2 <= B if CEB2 (cleared by RSTB);
9	// P <= f(OPMODE, A2*B2, C, P) if CEP (cleared by RSTP). A, B to P: 2 cycles; C and OPMODE to P: 1 cycle
10	// (they are not registered). P wraps at 48 bits. OPMODE is restricted to the eight values below; on
11	// any other value at an edge that loads P, P becomes X (the mode does not define it).
12	// Signed two's complement throughout, as the real block; unsigned data must have a zero top bit on
13	// each operand. Every register starts at 0: AMD's simulation model starts its registers at 0 in the
14	// `initial` block(s) cited below (Vivado 2024.1). Resets are synchronous, active high, clear only the
15	// register(s) they name and win over that register's clock enable. Timing figures and tile area are
16	// this project's arch's own, not AMD's. One block per DSP tile.
17	module dsp48e2_macc (
18	    input CLK,
19	    input [26:0] A,
20	    input [17:0] B,
21	    input [47:0] C,
22	    input [8:0] OPMODE,
23	    input RSTA, RSTB, RSTP, CEA2, CEB2, CEP,
24	    output [47:0] P
25	);
26	    reg [26:0] a2 = 0;
27	    reg [17:0] b2 = 0;
28	    reg [47:0] p = 0;
29	    wire signed [44:0] m = $signed(a2) * $signed(b2);       // signed 27x18 -> 45, exact
30	    wire [47:0] mx = {{3{m[44]}}, m};
31	    // what P loads, as a function so that it is defined from time 0
32	    function [47:0] alu;
33	        input [8:0] opmode;
34	        input [47:0] p;
35	        input [47:0] C;
36	        input [47:0] mx;
37	        case (opmode)
38	            9'b000000101: alu = mx;            // multiply
39	            9'b000100101: alu = p + mx;        // multiply-accumulate (Z = P)
40	            9'b000110101: alu = C + mx;        // multiply-add (Z = C)
41	            9'b010110101: alu = p + C + mx;    // multiply-add with P (W = P, Z = C)
42	            9'b110000000: alu = C;             // load C (W = C)
43	            9'b000110000: alu = C;             // load C (Z = C)
44	            9'b010000000: alu = p;             // hold through the ALU (W = P)
45	            9'b000000000: alu = 48'd0;         // zero
46	            default:      alu = {48{1'bx}};    // not a value of this mode
47	        endcase
48	    endfunction
49	    wire [47:0] nxt = alu(OPMODE, p, C, mx);
50	    always @(posedge CLK) begin
51	        if (RSTA) a2 <= 0; else if (CEA2) a2 <= A;
52	        if (RSTB) b2 <= 0; else if (CEB2) b2 <= B;
53	        if (RSTP) p <= 0; else if (CEP) p <= nxt;
54	    end
55	    assign P = p;
56	endmodule

Task

You are the planner. Read the design source, conv_layer.v, and decide which of the candidate arithmetic regions listed below to rewrite onto the hard block described next, or that none should be rewritten. Write a plan for the editor, who carries it out; do not edit any file.

The block

This run offered 2 blocks (dsp48e2_macc and dsp48e2_preadd_ma). Your first approved plan chose dsp48e2_macc, described below, and the choice is fixed for the rest of the run: every plan from here on uses dsp48e2_macc. The header below has no BLOCK: line; do not write one, even where an earlier plan shown here has it.

  • primitive to instantiate: dsp48e2_macc (precision INT27)
  • what it is: this mode copies the AMD DSP48E2 block in one configuration, the one-slice "27x18 + C MULT/MACC" row of UG579 v1.11 Table 2-12 (p46); OPMODE Tables 2-3 to 2-6 (p30). It is not the whole DSP48E2: only the ports below exist, and the rest of the real block is fixed inside the mode (ALUMODE = 0000, INMODE = 00000, CARRYINSEL = 000, CARRYIN = 0, D = 0, A[29:27] = 0, every cascade input 0, every clock enable and reset not listed as a port 0). Differences from the real block: one slice per tile where AMD's tile has two; A is 27 bits: A[29:27] serve only the A:B path, which is outside the mode; OPMODE is restricted to the listed values; PCOUT, ACOUT, BCOUT, the carry, pattern-detect and XOR outputs are not exposed; timing figures and tile area are this project's, not AMD's.
  • computes: P = A*B + C. That is one of the forms the block takes: OPMODE chooses the form at every clock edge (listed below).
  • input ports: CLK[1] (clock), A[27], B[18], C[48], OPMODE[9], RSTA[1], RSTB[1], RSTP[1], CEA2[1], CEB2[1], CEP[1]
  • output ports: P[48]
  • arithmetic: the block's operands and result are signed two's complement. A design operand narrower than its port must be widened to the port width: zero-extended if the design treats it as unsigned, sign-extended if signed. For unsigned data the top bit of each multiplier operand must be zero (UG579 v1.11 p38: "The multiplier can emulate unsigned math by setting the MSB of an input operand to zero."). AMD's words end with that sentence; the limits that follow are this project's, derived from it. So an unsigned operand may be at most 26 bits on A and 17 bits on B; a wider unsigned operand has no exact representation on this block. C and P are 48-bit values added modulo 2^48; extend a narrower addend to 48 bits by its own signedness. The result wraps at 48 bits: a value past the result width keeps its low 48 bits (modulo 2^48), with no saturation and no flag.
  • registers: on CLK, the block has exactly these registers. A2 loads A at a clock edge where CEA2 is high, and is cleared to 0 by RSTA. B2 loads B at a clock edge where CEB2 is high, and is cleared to 0 by RSTB. P loads the value OPMODE selects from A2*B2, C and P at a clock edge where CEP is high, and is cleared to 0 by RSTP; the output P is this register. P is always registered; C and OPMODE reach P without an input register.
  • timing: A and B reach P 2 clock edges after they are applied (applied before edge k, in P after edge k+1); C and OPMODE have no register before the one that drives P: the value present at a clock edge is in P after that edge (1 cycle).

  • control OPMODE: a per-cycle input, not registered; it acts at the clock edge that loads P, with the C value present at that edge. Legal values, and what the clock edge then does:

    • 000000101 (multiply): P <= A2*B2
    • 000100101 (multiply-accumulate): P <= P + A2*B2
    • 000110101 (multiply-add): P <= C + A2*B2
    • 010110101 (multiply-add with the running sum): P <= P + C + A2*B2
    • 110000000 (load C): P <= C
    • 000110000 (load C (second encoding)): P <= C
    • 010000000 (hold): P <= P
    • 000000000 (zero): P <= 0
    • any other value: a clock edge that loads P under it leaves P unknown (X); the mode does not define it.
  • resets and clock enables (resets RSTA, RSTB and RSTP; clock enables CEA2, CEB2 and CEP): each reset is synchronous and active high, clears only its own register to 0 at the clock edge that samples it high, and wins over that register's clock enable; each clock enable is active high; its register holds its value at a clock edge that samples the enable low. The block has no other reset and no other enable.
  • power-up: every register of the block starts at 0 (as the vendor's simulation model starts them), so P is defined from the first clock edge with or without a reset.
  • connections: connect every input port, by the port names above. A reset the rewrite does not need is tied to 0; a clock enable it does not need is tied to 1; OPMODE may be a constant legal value.
The block on this architecture

  • Blocks in series are separate stages. A path into C and OPMODE is not cut at the block's pins: it runs through the block to the register behind P, so the logic that drives them and the block's delay are in one stage.
  • A block of this mode takes a DSP tile alone.

Candidate regions

Multiplies in the unmodified conv_layer, counted in its packed netlist at seed 1: 84 on 84 DSP tiles, all in two_mult_18x19 mode (1 to a tile). The DSP tile count is the same at each of the five seeds. Multiplies in soft logic, if any, are not in this count.

An operand's bits are the bits it carries, the zero or sign extension to the width of the expression left out; signed or unsigned is how the multiply reads it. Correctness: these must hold
  • Same behavior. The rewritten design must produce the same outputs as the original on every cycle, with every register of the original starting at 0. Do not change what the design computes. Logic added only to reproduce it (recomputing a value, deriving the block's resets, clock enables or per-cycle control, correcting a block's result) is allowed.
  • Visibility. Only what the block's output ports carry is visible outside it. A value read by anything else (another expression, a module port, another module) cannot go inside a block: keep it outside, or recompute it. Check the source for every reader.
  • Terms. Keep every term of the original arithmetic. A term with no matching block port is computed outside the block. A port the design ties to a constant may be tied the same way on the block.
  • Registers and reset. The block's registers, if it has any, behave as the contract states. A design register may become a block register only if that keeps its behavior. Drive the block's resets (RSTA, RSTB and RSTP), clock enables (CEA2, CEB2 and CEP) and per-cycle control (OPMODE) so that "same behavior" holds after every reset of the design, not only at start-up. Registers the rewrite adds in soft logic start unknown; do not rely on their initial value. The block's own registers start at 0, as its power-up line states.
  • Assumptions. If the rewrite is correct only under an assumption about the design's inputs or use (a bound on a value, when a signal is asserted), state it on a line starting ASSUMES: and cite the lines of the design that support it. The reference testbench does not know your assumption, so the rewrite must still match the original on the testbench's stimulus, which does not respect it.

What is currently built and measured

The kept candidate is revision 1. conv_layer.v in this directory is the unmodified source as given to you, which the plan you write now rewrites afresh; the kept candidate's plan, shown next, was applied to that same source, and the diff after it is that edit.

The kept candidate's plan

REGIONS: qmult.$mul$conv_layer.v:2854$339
SHARED_REDUCTION: seq_mac's accumulator add_out_reg (the qadd sum), one per seq_mac instance
BLOCKS: 84
MAPPING: per seq_mac instance (84 instances, one block each): A = {11'b0, a_flopped}, B = {2'b0, b_flopped}, C = {31'b0, cload_reg} (new 17-bit soft register, non-zero only on the cycle after an eltwise-add edge), OPMODE = constant 9'b010110101 (P <= P + C + A2*B2), CLK = clk, RSTA = RSTB = reset, RSTP = reset | (eltwise_mode & eltwise_add), CEA2 = CEB2 = CEP = 1'b1; the visible add_out_reg = P[31:0] | {15'b0, cload_reg}, which feeds the unchanged saturation/downcast logic and out

PLAN:

What the measurement showed. The kept rewrite's critical path is the per-cycle OPMODE path: the control flop that drives op (0.060) + 1.702 ns of routing to the PE + one LUT (0.153, the ~sel term) + 1.314 ns of routing + the block's 2.140 + 0.019 setup = 5.41 ns. OPMODE and C are the only block inputs whose path is not cut at the block pin; everything driven from a global control signal into OPMODE therefore pays routing-to-the-PE plus the full 2.159 ns block delay. RSTA/RSTB/RSTP/CEx/A/B, by contrast, end at a block register (setup 0.019). This revision keeps the same block and the same 84 instances but moves all dependence on eltwise_mode/eltwise_add off OPMODE and onto RSTP (a cheap endpoint) and onto a local register that drives C with no logic in between. OPMODE becomes a constant, so the long control route no longer runs through the block.

Candidate regions. One candidate, qmult.$mul$conv_layer.v:2854$339; converted in all 84 instances of qmult (one per seq_mac, instantiated from the PE at conv_layer.v:2648). None left. 84 blocks, one DSP tile each, within the device's 306; the unmodified design already uses 84 DSP tiles.

The idea. Write A(n) for the original add_out_reg during cycle n, r(n) for reset, s(n) for eltwise_mode & eltwise_add, S(n) = a_flopped(n)+b_flopped(n) (17 bits), M(n) for mul_out_reg(n). The original (lines 2804-2818) is

A(n+1) = r(n) ? 0 : ( s(n) ? S(n) : A(n) + M(n) ).

Instead of switching OPMODE between accumulate and load-C, clear the block's accumulator with RSTP on an eltwise-add edge and carry the loaded value S(n) in a soft 17-bit register cload_reg for the one cycle it is live; the block adds it back into the accumulator at the next edge through C, with the constant OPMODE P <= P + C + A2*B2. Because cload_reg is non-zero only on a cycle where RSTP fired at the previous edge, P is 0 exactly on those cycles, so the architectural value is P | cload_reg — a bitwise OR, not an adder (the two are never both non-zero). The 32-bit soft adder and the 32-bit mux of the original stay deleted.

Formally, with RSTP = r|s, OPMODE constant, C = {31'b0, cload_reg}:

P(n+1) = (r(n)|s(n)) ? 0 : P(n) + cload_reg(n) + M(n) (48-bit, low 32 bits used)
cload_reg(n+1) = r(n) ? 0 : ( s(n) ? S(n) : 0 )
V(n) = P(n)[31:0] | {15'b0, cload_reg(n)}

Invariants: cload_reg(n) != 0 implies s(n-1) & ~r(n-1), which implies RSTP was high at edge n-1, so P(n) = 0; hence V(n) = P(n) + cload_reg(n) always. Induction on V(n) = A(n):

  • r(n)=1: P(n+1)=0, cload_reg(n+1)=0, V=0 = A(n+1).
  • r(n)=0, s(n)=1: P(n+1)=0 (RSTP), cload_reg(n+1)=S(n), V = S(n) = A(n+1).
  • r(n)=0, s(n)=0: P(n+1) = P(n)+cload_reg(n)+M(n) ≡ A(n)+M(n), cload_reg(n+1)=0, V(n+1) = A(n+1). The block adds modulo 2^48 and the design modulo 2^32; the low 32 bits of a sum depend only on the low 32 bits of the addends, and only P[31:0] is read, so the wrap matches.

M(n) = A2(n)*B2(n) equals the original mul_out_reg(n) because RSTA/RSTB = reset clears A2/B2 on exactly the edges that cleared mul_out_reg (and 0*0 = 0), and CEA2/CEB2 = 1 as mul_out_reg always loaded.

Edits for the editor, inside module seq_mac only (conv_layer.v:2765-2846).

  1. Keep the a_flopped/b_flopped always block (lines 2783-2791) exactly as it is.
  2. Delete: the qmult instance (line 2793); mul_out/mul_out_reg and their always block (lines 2780-2781, 2795-2801); add_in1/add_in2 and their assigns (lines 2804-2808); the qadd instance (line 2810); add_out (line 2775); the add_out_reg register and its always block (lines 2806, 2812-2818). Leave the qmult and qadd module definitions (lines 2849-2866) in the file; nothing else instantiates them.
  3. Add, in place of the deleted logic:
    ```verilog
    wire sel = eltwise_mode & eltwise_add;

//carries the eltwise-add load value for the one cycle it is live
reg [`DWIDTH:0] cload_reg;
always @(posedge clk) begin
if (reset) cload_reg <= 0;
else if (sel) cload_reg <= {1'b0, a_flopped} + {1'b0, b_flopped};
else cload_reg <= 0;
end

wire [47:0] dsp_p;
dsp48e2_macc mac_dsp (
.CLK(clk),
.A({{(27-`DWIDTH){1'b0}}, a_flopped}),
.B({{(18-`DWIDTH){1'b0}}, b_flopped}),
.C({{(48-`DWIDTH-1){1'b0}}, cload_reg}),
.OPMODE(9'b010110101),
.RSTA(reset), .RSTB(reset), .RSTP(reset | sel),
.CEA2(1'b1), .CEB2(1'b1), .CEP(1'b1),
.P(dsp_p)
);

wire [2*`DWIDTH-1:0] add_out_reg =
dsp_p[2*DWIDTH-1:0] | {{(DWIDTH-1){1'b0}}, cload_reg};

`OPMODE` is the literal constant `9'b010110101`, a legal value of the mode (`P <= P + C + A2*B2`), tied the same on every cycle, so `P` is never X and no design signal reaches `OPMODE`.

4. Leave the `mac_out` saturation assign (lines 2823-2835) and the `out` register (lines 2838-2844) untouched; they read the new `add_out_reg` wire.

**Register stages, operands to result.** MAC path before: `a`/`b` → `a_flopped`/`b_flopped` (1) → multiply → `mul_out_reg` (2) → add/mux → `add_out_reg` (3) → saturation → `out` (4). After: `a`/`b` → `a_flopped`/`b_flopped` soft (1) → block `A2`/`B2` (2) → block `P` (3) → OR + saturation → `out` soft (4). Same four stages. Eltwise-add path before: `a`/`b` → `a_flopped`/`b_flopped` (1) → zero-extend/add/mux → `add_out_reg` (2) → saturation → `out` (3). After: `a`/`b` → `a_flopped`/`b_flopped` (1) → 17-bit add → `cload_reg` (2) → OR + saturation → `out` (3). Same three stages; the loaded value never passes through `P`.

**Why this should be faster.** The only paths into the block that carry design logic are now: `C`, driven straight off `cload_reg` with no intervening LUT (flop 0.060 + one route + 2.159); and `RSTP`/`CEx`/`A`/`B`, which end at a block register (the long control route + one LUT + 0.019 ≈ 3.3 ns worst case on `reset | sel`). The 5.41 ns `OPMODE`-through-the-block path disappears. The new output-side term is the 17-bit OR between `P` and the saturation logic; its low-bit OR merges into the saturation mux LUT and its high bits merge into the existing `|add_out_reg[30:15]` reduction, so the logic depth from `P` to `out` should be unchanged.

**Area.** Versus the kept candidate: `sum_reg` (17 flops, unconditional load) is replaced by `cload_reg` (17 flops, enable plus zero-load) and its 17-bit adder moves from in front of the register to in front of it as well — the same order of soft logic; the added OR is 17 LUT inputs that fold into existing LUTs. No 32-bit soft adder, no 32-bit mux and no 32-bit `add_out_reg` register return. DSP tiles stay at 84.

**Correctness notes.** Visibility: `mul_out` and `add_out` have no reader outside the registers that consumed them (lines 2780-2818), so nothing hidden in the block is read elsewhere; `a_flopped`/`b_flopped` remain soft registers because `A2`/`B2` are not readable and `cload_reg`'s adder needs them; the architectural `add_out_reg` leaves the block on `P` (ORed with `cload_reg`). Resets: `RSTA`/`RSTB`/`RSTP` are driven so the block registers clear on exactly the edges the original's registers cleared, and `RSTP` wins over `CEP` just as the original's `if (reset)` won over its mux; mid-run resets of arbitrary length are covered by the induction above, which is over every cycle. Power-up/X: block registers start at 0; `cload_reg` is a new soft register and starts unknown, but it is cleared at every edge of the testbench's 4-cycle reset phase and can reach `P` only through `C`, while `RSTP` is high at each of those edges, so no X reaches `P` or `out`, and comparison begins after the reset release. Widths: both multiplier operands are 16-bit unsigned, zero-extended, 16 ≤ 26 on `A` and 16 ≤ 17 on `B`, so each operand's top bit is 0 and the 32-bit product is exact; `C` is a zero-extended 17-bit value.

No assumption about the design's inputs is needed; the rewrite is unconditionally equivalent, including when `eltwise_mode`/`eltwise_add`/`reset` toggle arbitrarily.

DESIGN_CHANGE: if `seq_mac` sampled `eltwise_mode`/`eltwise_add` into a local register one cycle before the edge on which the eltwise load takes effect (as the PE's `ready_for_eltwise_op` sequencing at lines 2647-2656 effectively already guarantees), the mode select could drive `OPMODE` straight from a flop, letting the block's own load-C mode replace `cload_reg`, the `RSTP` term and the output OR.

Reference simulation

After the edit, the framework simulates the edited design against the original with the reference testbench, comparing every output of seq_mac on every cycle after the testbench's reset phase (its first 4 cycles). A candidate that does not match is not measured. The testbench (tb_equiv_seq_mac.v) and the block's behavioural model (dsp48e2_macc.v) are in sim_reference/.

Read these files in this directory: conv_layer.v

response

I'll read the source first.

REGIONS: NONE
SHARED_REDUCTION: NONE
BLOCKS: 0
MAPPING: none — this pass instantiates no block; the kept revision 1 mapping (one dsp48e2_macc per seq_mac, A={11'b0,a_flopped}, B={2'b0,b_flopped}, C={31'b0,cload_reg}, constant OPMODE=9'b010110101, RSTA=RSTB=reset, RSTP=reset|sel, CEA2=CEB2=CEP=1, visible add_out_reg=P[31:0]|cload_reg) stands unchanged.

PLAN:

Candidate regions. There is exactly one candidate, qmult.$mul$conv_layer.v:2854$339, and the kept revision already converts it in all 84 instances of qmult (one per seq_mac, one seq_mac per processing_element, 12 live PEs per matmul_4x4_systolic at lines 2570-2588 × 7 matmuls at lines 1309-1549 = 84). Nothing is left unconverted for a correctness reason; coverage of the candidate list is 100%. I propose no new rewrite this pass because the two levers that remain — the block's soft surround and the measured critical path — are both at a floor that this block cannot move, as follows.

The measured critical path no longer touches the block. In the revision-1 timing report both endpoints are soft flops ($sdffe~36^Q~0.Q launches, $sdff~338^Q~1.D captures) and the path is four .names levels; no DSP pin appears on it. Of its 5.042 ns of arrival, 4.306 ns is wire (0.868 + 1.286 + 0.868 + 1.284) and only 0.612 ns is cells. Such per-hop wire delays are chip-crossing, not intra-PE: every signal that enters or leaves the block in the kept rewrite (A, B, C, RSTP, P) is local to one PE and its DSP column. The four-level shape matches the memory-qualification broadcast in systolic_data_setup: a_mem_access_counter (lines 2299-2311, a sync-reset-plus-enable flop) → the four a_mem_access_counter==k comparisons against validity_mask_a_cols (lines 2314-2319, 8-bit equalities, two levels) → the a_data_valid OR → the a_data & {DWIDTH{a_data_valid}} & {DWIDTH{validity_mask_a_rows[i]}} qualification merged with the eltwise_mode ? select (lines 2325-2328, 2356-2358) → in_a of a PE → a_flopped (a plain sync-reset flop, line 2783). The same chain exists bit-for-bit in the unmodified design; it is control/datapath-qualification logic, not an arithmetic region, so no mapping of the multiply onto dsp48e2_macc can shorten it. For comparison, the longest path the rewrite itself owns is P → the 16-input |add_out_reg[30:15] reduction (two 6-LUT levels) → the mac_out select (one level, 4 inputs: add_out_reg[31], the overflow flag, dsp_p[i], cload_reg[i]) → out: three levels of short, DSP-adjacent wire, i.e. strictly shallower than the path that sets the period. Shaving a level there (I costed two ways of doing it: pre-ORing cload_reg[16:15] into a separate flag, and a registered pre-saturated eltwise value) buys nothing while the 4-level control chain is critical, and the second way costs 16 flip-flops per instance, about +8% area.

The soft logic around the block is already minimal for this block. Per seq_mac the rewrite leaves 65 flops (a_flopped/b_flopped 32, cload_reg 17, out 16) plus a 17-bit add and the saturation/downcast. Each is forced:

  • a_flopped/b_flopped cannot move into A2/B2. The original needs three register stages from a,b to the accumulator value (a_flopped → mul_out_reg → add_out_reg, lines 2783-2818); the block supplies only two (A2 then P). Supplying the third after the block instead of before it makes P hold add_out_reg of a future cycle, so the edge at which RSTP/the eltwise load must act would have to be driven by reset/eltwise_* values one cycle before they exist — not causal. Supplying it on only one operand gives the wrong product. So one soft 32-flop stage before A/B is required, and it is also what the eltwise adder reads.
  • cload_reg cannot be removed. At an eltwise-add edge the architectural accumulator must become a_flopped+b_flopped, a value the block cannot form (P is A2*B2 plus C or P, and B2 would have to be forced to 1 one cycle early). With OPMODE constant the loaded value must therefore live one cycle in soft logic and re-enter through C; 17 bits is the exact width, and the mux into it folds into the flop's own enable/reset.
  • The 17-bit adder and the saturation/downcast (lines 2823-2835) have no block port and must stay outside; out is the module's only output register.
  • Making OPMODE per-cycle is already measured worse. The first pass did exactly that and came in at 5.389 ns: OPMODE has no input register, so its driver's chip-crossing route plus the block's 2.140 ns are one stage. A variant I checked that drives OPMODE[4] from a local registered sel (accumulate 000100101 vs multiply-add 000110101, with C tied straight to cload_reg) keeps P valid only on non-load cycles, so the output mux gains an input and a level, and it adds a flop — deeper and larger, not better.

Register stages (the kept rewrite, unchanged). MAC path, before: a/b → a_flopped/b_flopped (1) → mul_out_reg (2) → add_out_reg (3) → out (4). After: a/b → a_flopped/b_flopped soft (1) → A2/B2 (2) → P (3) → out soft (4). Eltwise-add path, before: a/b → a_flopped/b_flopped (1) → add_out_reg (2) → out (3). After: a/b → a_flopped/b_flopped (1) → cload_reg (2) → out (3). Same counts on both paths.

Result to keep. Revision 1: 84 blocks on 84 DSP tiles (no more tiles than the unmodified design, and within the 306 available), area 5.0513e+07, period 5.01801 ns, product 2.53475e+08, −27.39% against the baseline's 3.49095e+08, which is well outside the ~3.7% seed spread, so its median over all five seeds should stay below the original's.

DESIGN_CHANGE: the period is now set by systolic_data_setup's memory-qualification broadcast, not by any arithmetic. a_mem_access_counter/b_mem_access_counter are free-running counters (lines 2299-2311, 2384-2395), so a_data_valid/b_data_valid (lines 2314-2319, 2398-2403) can be computed from the counters' next values and registered, leaving a single LUT (a_data & valid & mask, merged with the eltwise_mode select of lines 2356-2358) between the BRAM data and the PE array instead of four levels on a chip-crossing route. That would cut roughly one to three LUT-plus-wire hops from the design's own critical path without changing when data is qualified.

DESIGN_CHANGE: if seq_mac saw the eltwise select one cycle before the edge on which the load takes effect (the PE's ready_for_eltwise_op sequencing at lines 2646-2648 effectively already establishes it), OPMODE could be driven from a local flop and the block's own load-C mode would replace cload_reg, the 17-bit adder, the RSTP term and the output OR — about 17 flops and 17 LUTs per instance, roughly 10% of the design's area.

Offered and packed are not the same unit: a site is a source region, a packed block is an elaborated instance, and one block can fuse several sites (or the editor can chain several blocks for one site), so they are recorded raw rather than as a ratio.

Objective loop

2 rewrite(s) measured · stopped by: planner declined
revisions allowed
9

Pass 2

planner declined (no representative mapping)
objective
area x delay (delay register to register, 2026-11-02)
baseline
layout koios_small_sdc · used area 6.14515e+07 · critical path 5.68082 ns · wirelength 285909 · area x delay (delay register to register, 2026-11-02) 3.49095e+08 (read from entry; median per term over the entry's seed runs (its seed_medians; seeds lists them))

The objective is area x delay (delay register to register, 2026-11-02): A times D, from VPR's total used logic block area (I/Os and empty grid tiles excluded), A, the delay D (the register-to-register figure since 2026-10-20) and the total routed wirelength, W (all three recorded under every objective). Each term is the median over the loop's seeds; the objective is formed from the medians. Beside every product stand the ratios against the baseline, A/A0 (area), D/D0 (critical path), W/W0 (wirelength) and their product, so the normalization can be read off. After each measured rewrite, the planner is given every measurement so far and asked for a revision. A revision is built and checked like the first rewrite, and the lowest product is kept, a later rewrite having to beat it by more than 3.70%. The loop stops when the planner declines to revise, when a revision does not lower the best product by more than 3.70%, when a revision fails its checks, or when 9 revisions have been made.

Stated: the BLOCKS, TILES and STAGE_NS lines of the plan header. Measured: the packed DSP tile count from the gate flow, and the median period, area and wirelength over the seeds. "A/A0 · D/D0 · W/W0" are the rewrite's medians over the baseline's, whose product is the "vs baseline" change plus one. "vs previous" is against the rewrite measured before this one. "Path 1 ends in" is read from the rewrite's own timing report and netlist: the constant coefficients on the block (or plain multiply) that path 1 passes through, matched against the constants each module of the edited RTL multiplies by; two modules that share a coefficient set are both named, and the hover text gives the element and its constants. Verification comes from verification.yaml in the run dir when one was written; the loop itself does not run the equivalence checks. Its label is proven when the LEC closed, simulation under assumption when the simulation passed without a closed proof and the plan stated a bound it rests on, simulation only when it stated none; the hover text gives the simulation and LEC results and the stated bounds.

I'll read the source first.

REGIONS: NONE
SHARED_REDUCTION: NONE
BLOCKS: 0
MAPPING: none — this pass instantiates no block; the kept revision 1 mapping (one dsp48e2_macc per seq_mac, A={11'b0,a_flopped}, B={2'b0,b_flopped}, C={31'b0,cload_reg}, constant OPMODE=9'b010110101, RSTA=RSTB=reset, RSTP=reset|sel, CEA2=CEB2=CEP=1, visible add_out_reg=P[31:0]|cload_reg) stands unchanged.

PLAN:

Candidate regions. There is exactly one candidate, qmult.$mul$conv_layer.v:2854$339, and the kept revision already converts it in all 84 instances of qmult (one per seq_mac, one seq_mac per processing_element, 12 live PEs per matmul_4x4_systolic at lines 2570-2588 × 7 matmuls at lines 1309-1549 = 84). Nothing is left unconverted for a correctness reason; coverage of the candidate list is 100%. I propose no new rewrite this pass because the two levers that remain — the block's soft surround and the measured critical path — are both at a floor that this block cannot move, as follows.

The measured critical path no longer touches the block. In the revision-1 timing report both endpoints are soft flops ($sdffe~36^Q~0.Q launches, $sdff~338^Q~1.D captures) and the path is four .names levels; no DSP pin appears on it. Of its 5.042 ns of arrival, 4.306 ns is wire (0.868 + 1.286 + 0.868 + 1.284) and only 0.612 ns is cells. Such per-hop wire delays are chip-crossing, not intra-PE: every signal that enters or leaves the block in the kept rewrite (A, B, C, RSTP, P) is local to one PE and its DSP column. The four-level shape matches the memory-qualification broadcast in systolic_data_setup: a_mem_access_counter (lines 2299-2311, a sync-reset-plus-enable flop) → the four a_mem_access_counter==k comparisons against validity_mask_a_cols (lines 2314-2319, 8-bit equalities, two levels) → the a_data_valid OR → the a_data & {DWIDTH{a_data_valid}} & {DWIDTH{validity_mask_a_rows[i]}} qualification merged with the eltwise_mode ? select (lines 2325-2328, 2356-2358) → in_a of a PE → a_flopped (a plain sync-reset flop, line 2783). The same chain exists bit-for-bit in the unmodified design; it is control/datapath-qualification logic, not an arithmetic region, so no mapping of the multiply onto dsp48e2_macc can shorten it. For comparison, the longest path the rewrite itself owns is P → the 16-input |add_out_reg[30:15] reduction (two 6-LUT levels) → the mac_out select (one level, 4 inputs: add_out_reg[31], the overflow flag, dsp_p[i], cload_reg[i]) → out: three levels of short, DSP-adjacent wire, i.e. strictly shallower than the path that sets the period. Shaving a level there (I costed two ways of doing it: pre-ORing cload_reg[16:15] into a separate flag, and a registered pre-saturated eltwise value) buys nothing while the 4-level control chain is critical, and the second way costs 16 flip-flops per instance, about +8% area.

The soft logic around the block is already minimal for this block. Per seq_mac the rewrite leaves 65 flops (a_flopped/b_flopped 32, cload_reg 17, out 16) plus a 17-bit add and the saturation/downcast. Each is forced:

  • a_flopped/b_flopped cannot move into A2/B2. The original needs three register stages from a,b to the accumulator value (a_flopped → mul_out_reg → add_out_reg, lines 2783-2818); the block supplies only two (A2 then P). Supplying the third after the block instead of before it makes P hold add_out_reg of a future cycle, so the edge at which RSTP/the eltwise load must act would have to be driven by reset/eltwise_* values one cycle before they exist — not causal. Supplying it on only one operand gives the wrong product. So one soft 32-flop stage before A/B is required, and it is also what the eltwise adder reads.
  • cload_reg cannot be removed. At an eltwise-add edge the architectural accumulator must become a_flopped+b_flopped, a value the block cannot form (P is A2*B2 plus C or P, and B2 would have to be forced to 1 one cycle early). With OPMODE constant the loaded value must therefore live one cycle in soft logic and re-enter through C; 17 bits is the exact width, and the mux into it folds into the flop's own enable/reset.
  • The 17-bit adder and the saturation/downcast (lines 2823-2835) have no block port and must stay outside; out is the module's only output register.
  • Making OPMODE per-cycle is already measured worse. The first pass did exactly that and came in at 5.389 ns: OPMODE has no input register, so its driver's chip-crossing route plus the block's 2.140 ns are one stage. A variant I checked that drives OPMODE[4] from a local registered sel (accumulate 000100101 vs multiply-add 000110101, with C tied straight to cload_reg) keeps P valid only on non-load cycles, so the output mux gains an input and a level, and it adds a flop — deeper and larger, not better.

Register stages (the kept rewrite, unchanged). MAC path, before: a/b → a_flopped/b_flopped (1) → mul_out_reg (2) → add_out_reg (3) → out (4). After: a/b → a_flopped/b_flopped soft (1) → A2/B2 (2) → P (3) → out soft (4). Eltwise-add path, before: a/b → a_flopped/b_flopped (1) → add_out_reg (2) → out (3). After: a/b → a_flopped/b_flopped (1) → cload_reg (2) → out (3). Same counts on both paths.

Result to keep. Revision 1: 84 blocks on 84 DSP tiles (no more tiles than the unmodified design, and within the 306 available), area 5.0513e+07, period 5.01801 ns, product 2.53475e+08, −27.39% against the baseline's 3.49095e+08, which is well outside the ~3.7% seed spread, so its median over all five seeds should stay below the original's.

DESIGN_CHANGE: the period is now set by systolic_data_setup's memory-qualification broadcast, not by any arithmetic. a_mem_access_counter/b_mem_access_counter are free-running counters (lines 2299-2311, 2384-2395), so a_data_valid/b_data_valid (lines 2314-2319, 2398-2403) can be computed from the counters' next values and registered, leaving a single LUT (a_data & valid & mask, merged with the eltwise_mode select of lines 2356-2358) between the BRAM data and the PE array instead of four levels on a chip-crossing route. That would cut roughly one to three LUT-plus-wire hops from the design's own critical path without changing when data is qualified.

DESIGN_CHANGE: if seq_mac saw the eltwise select one cycle before the edge on which the load takes effect (the PE's ready_for_eltwise_op sequencing at lines 2646-2648 effectively already establishes it), OPMODE could be driven from a local flop and the block's own load-C mode would replace cload_reg, the 17-bit adder, the RSTP term and the output OR — about 17 flops and 17 LUTs per instance, roughly 10% of the design's area.
